ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u156	Thank you very much. Please, Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan, Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u157	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle, I present my position on the report of the committee on the draft law amending the Water Law Act. The amendments introduced are a response to the interpretation doubts raised around the provisions of the Law of 20 July 2017. The provisions introduced by the new law obliged the authorities competent to apply the new provisions also with regard to proceedings which are pending and were initiated on the basis of previous provisions. This legal situation causes many problems. The competent authorities are faced with the question of whether the procedural actions that were carried out prior to the entry into force of the new law, e.g. on the issue of a provision on the absence of the need for an environmental impact assessment, it should be repeated. This is due to the fact that the provisions of the Law of 20 July 2017 have obliged the bodies of the first and second instance which are conducting proceedings in matters of environmental decisions to obtain from the competent authority in matters of water law assessments the opinions and arrangements which have been introduced under the new law. This legal situation poses enormous difficulties in administrative proceedings which were granted running under the provisions prior to the date of entry into force of the new Water Law. There are many obstacles to this, among others. there is a need to return the documentation and to supplement the positions of the authorities on water law assessments after the investigation has been completed. Because of the implementation of the above mentioned above. the operation is not possible to deal with matters within the statutory deadline. This is very burdensome for investors. Projectors respond to expectations and propose to amend the transitional provisions so that in cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act of 20 July 2017. Water law concerning environmental decisions and environmental impact assessments of the company, the provisions in force before 1 January 2018 as well as the transitional provisions of the Act of 15 October 2015 amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments and certain other laws. The changes made will certainly meet with a positive reception of investors waiting for a timely investment decision. Proceedings before 1 January 2018 shall be deprived of the need to obtain opinions or arrangements from the competent authority in matters of water law assessments which have been introduced pursuant to Article. This Decision is addressed to the Member States. About Free and Solidarni will be in favour of the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u195	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs the report of the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Civil Records and the Law on Civil Status Acts, Print No 2236 and 2272. At its meeting on 26 February, the Commission carried out its first reading of this draft law. The draft law provides for amendments to the Act of 24 September 2010 on population records and in the Act of 28 November 2014. The Law on civil status records in order to add to the data in the birth certificate of the parents' PESEL numbers, which will be introduced when the acts are created in the Civil Service Base, and the addition of the PESEL numbers of parents, if assigned, to the scope of the collected data in the PESEL register. This will provide clear information on the relationship between the person and her parents included in the birth certificate. This amendment aims to facilitate the provision of electronic services for which such data are required. This will enable users of data collected in the PESEL register to collect data on these relationships, which will simplify the delivery process of benefits, to make it easier and faster for citizens benefiting from aid schemes. In addition, the project provides for the possibility of electronic notification of the birth of the child. The provisions introduced provide for the notification of birth in the form of an electronic document. This will relieve parents who should focus their attention on a new member of the family at the beginning, from the embarrassing need to appear personally at the civil status office and devote time to official activities. The birth certificate will also include a statement of name selection. Upon registration of birth by the head of the civil status office, a summary copy of the birth certificate shall be sent to the person submitting the birth certificate, the notification of the given PESEL number and the certificate of registration in paper or electronic form, depending on the choice of the person making the electronic birth declaration. The project also provides for the possibility of reporting in relations between entities using access to the PESEL registry and entities competent to record events in the PESEL register in a written form or in the form of an electronic document. Currently, users of such access, e.g. National Health Fund or Social Insurance Institution, in case of non-compliance notify them to the Minister responsible for IT. The introduction of provisions allowing notifications of non-compliance directly to the authority responsible for data registration will significantly speed up the compliance of the data with the facts. The project also introduced provisions concerning the provision of data from the civil status register to the following services: Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Central Anti-corruption Office, Military Counter-intelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service only to the extent necessary to carry out their statutory tasks. Finally, this proposal entails a change in the inclusion of the above mentioned State, Police, Military Police, Border Guard, Prison Service, in the catalogue of entities to which copies of the civil status act and certificates of the data on the person concerned are issued and not included in the register of civil status. These amendments are justified by the need for effective implementation of the statutory tasks of the special services. The Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication with the following exceptions: as regards the addition of parent PESEL numbers to the child's PESEL number, as from 1 December 2018; as regards the possibility of notification of non-compliance in electronic form, as from 1 May 2019; as regards the provisions concerning the electronic birth declaration of the child as from 1 June 2018. The Commission has introduced several legislative, clarification and regulation amendments to the proposal and has introduced one amendment to the Act amending the IT Act. This amendment aims to postpone the deadline for the implementation of this system by 30 May 2019 and not, as was the case in the draft, by 15 May 2018. The introduction of this amendment will provide the necessary time to complete the work to launch this ICT system. It should be stressed that, in the current state of the art of the design and implementation of the scheme, it is not possible to implement it within a statutory deadline, i.e. by 15 May 2018 Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I request the adoption of the attached bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The amended law offers such opportunities, which will have a positive impact on the burden of parents who have so far personally had to appear at the civil status office. The amendment of the Act aims to improve the clarification of notified non-compliances by authorities benefiting from access to the PESEL register. At the moment, information on non-compliance had to be notified to the Minister responsible for IT, which significantly prolonged the waiting time for the correctness of the data recorded in the register. The provisions introduced shall allow notifications of non-compliance to be communicated directly to the authority responsible for the register. The change will have an impact on shortening the timing of cases. The amended Act introduces provisions concerning the sharing of data in the civil status register to services such as Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Central Anti-corruption Office, Military Counter-intelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, but only for statutory tasks. It should be stressed that the changes made to the rules are essential for the implementation of the IT project on the construction and development of the system of state registers. ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u241	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products, contained in print No 2266. Today, the Committee on Agriculture carried out its first reading and examined the bill. Its purpose is to extend the scope of tasks of the Trade Quality Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles to the tasks of carrying out laboratory tests on samples of agri-food products for the implementation of the mechanisms of the common agricultural policy relating to the organisation of agricultural markets and to carrying out laboratory tests on samples of agri-food products for the implementation of the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. In carrying out its new tasks, the inspection will work with the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture under the mechanisms of the common agricultural policy and the National Agricultural Support Centre for the implementation of the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. This cooperation will take place on the basis of agreements concluded with the restructuring agency and the National Agricultural Support Centre. The requirement for laboratory testing of agri-food products in order to confirm their quality laid down in European Union legislation when implementing the mechanisms of the common agricultural policy entails the need for continuous laboratory analysis in eight mechanisms, inter alia. in intervention buying in and selling butter and skimmed milk powder, private storage aid for butter, intervention buying in and selling cereals, industrial sugar processing, private storage aid for pigmeat or private storage aid for skimmed milk powder. The implementation of the mechanism under the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived also entails the obligation to carry out laboratory tests on foodstuffs provided under aid to the poorest. The Agricultural Restructuring and Modernisation Agency and the National Agricultural Support Centre do not have their own laboratories. Therefore, given the need to carry out laboratory tests on samples of agri-food products under these mechanisms of the common agricultural policy and in the implementation of the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived, as well as to optimise the spending of funds for these purposes, the surveys should be carried out by the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection, as the inspection has modern, specialised and well-equipped laboratories that carry out research on all groups of agri-food commodities. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 of the bill is authorised for the President of the Council of Ministers to transfer to the Trade Quality Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles by means of a regulation of budgetary appropriations, which in the 2018 Budget Act have been secured for the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture to carry out samples of agri-food products in the framework of the implementation of CAP mechanisms and Part 44: Social security - to carry out sample tests in the framework of the implementation of the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. The project will not increase expenditure from the state budget or from the budgets of local government units, nor will it affect the competitiveness of the economy, in particular the functioning of small and medium-sized enterprises. The proposed date of entry into force of the Act, 7 days from the date of publication, results from the need to ensure continuity of laboratory testing of samples of agri-food products. No amendments were tabled in the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. The proposed changes are not controversial, they are necessary. Therefore, on behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I would like to ask the High Sejm to approve the Government bill in print 2266. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u243	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! High Izbo! The proposed law is an amendment to the Act of 21 December 2000 on the commercial quality of agri-food products. The purpose of this law is to add the tasks of the Trade Quality Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles related to the laboratory testing of samples of agri-food products for the implementation of the mechanisms of the common agricultural policy relating to the organisation of agricultural markets and to laboratory testing of samples of agri-food products for the implementation of the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. In carrying out these tasks, the inspection will cooperate with the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, as well as with the National Agricultural Support Centre. As part of the implementation of the CAP, it is necessary to carry out laboratory tests on samples of agri-food products taken. Such an obligation arises directly from Union rules which indicate that intervention products must meet certain quality parameters. The requirement for laboratory testing of agri-food products in order to confirm their quality laid down in European Union legislation when implementing CAP mechanisms requires continuous analysis in these mechanisms. The Agency and KOWR do not have their own laboratories. It is therefore necessary to sign the agreement, to cooperate with the inspection to be carried out by the restructuring agency and the KOWR. The relevant agreement will therefore be signed. Experience so far in cooperation with inspection shows that carrying out laboratory testing of samples of agri-food products by inspection is a solution that guarantees the best quality of the research carried out. These laboratories are of course modern, controlled and very well equipped. The project also does not increase expenditure from the state budget or from the budget of local government units, nor does it affect the competitiveness of the economy, in particular the functioning of small and medium-sized enterprises. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice advocates the adoption of a government draft law amending the Act on Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products from Printing No 2266. ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u260	Government draft law amending the Act on Commercial Quality of Agricultural and Food Products. The project concerns laboratory testing of samples of agri-food products for the implementation of common agricultural policy mechanisms for the organisation of agricultural markets and the implementation of operations co-financed by the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. At this level, the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection, which has been added to this obligation, will cooperate with the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture and with the National Agricultural Support Centre. The cooperation agreements which IJHARS will conclude with ARiMR and KOWR will specify, among others. mechanisms under which laboratory tests will be carried out, detailed scope and time limits for carrying out laboratory tests and detailed rules for the preparation and delivery of samples for laboratory tests. The IJHARS agreement with ARiMR will include provisions to ensure that ARiM meets the accreditation criteria as a paying agency. The Union rules set out the mechanisms of the common agricultural policy for the quality requirements of intervention products, the methods used to determine their quality, the methods of sampling and analysis. The project is therefore also to be adapted to the EU regulation in this area. According to the Ministry of Agriculture, the draft law was addressed to public consultation of national industry organisations and associations and organisations, but no comments were made within them. The implementation of the research this year will cost about PLN 2 million. At this stage, it is difficult to determine whether this will be sufficient. However, as the Ministry assures (and we hope that it will keep its word): The project will not increase expenditure from the state budget or from the budgets of local government units. It will not affect the competitiveness of the economy, especially the functioning of small and medium-sized enterprises. This law has been implemented rapidly, which should not be practiced in the legislative process of any legislation. The Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of the project.

The joint committee's report is contained in print No 2234. The first reading of the draft in print No 1042 took place at its 31st meeting on 2 December 2016, when the Civic Platform Club requested that it be rejected at first reading. However, this proposal has not been recognised by the High House, which has decided to transfer it to the Environment Committee and the Agriculture Committee. A subcommittee was set up to carry out detailed work on the project. High Izbo! Both the hunting economy and the estimation and the payment of hunting compensation have been controversial for years. To date, the way these tasks have been carried out has brought about the right dissatisfaction of farmers. Setting compensation by hunters, problems with payment of the sums due to farmers, lack of redress are legitimate reasons for dissatisfaction. On the other hand, we do not live today, in the 21st century, in a paradise garden. Man has interfered so strongly in nature that she alone is unable to maintain a balance between species. The man must perform a regulatory function and this function is being performed by hunters, especially in recent months, when Poland has struggled with African swine fever - still struggling with it - which requires a radical reduction in the number of boars living in our country by firing them off. It is these months that show how much people are needed to accomplish this task. High Izbo! During the work of the subcommittee, 35 amendments were removed from the project, art. 1, relating to the Hunting Law Act. It's about changing 1-4, 8.-11, 15. -33., 36. -41. And 43. -45. In the project there were only changes governing the setting of boundaries of hunting circuits by the sejmik voivodship, art. 27 Hunting rights, the right of the owner of the parcel to refuse the establishment of a hunting circuit on his property, which is in connection with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 10 July 2014, and the rules for the estimation and payment of hunting damages. The revised rules provide that an assessment will be carried out by a team consisting of a representative of a municipality body, i.e. a councillor or an official, a representative of a tenant or a manager of a hunting circuit and a farmer injured. If, at this stage, the parties reach an agreement, compensation shall be paid. If not, re-estimation will be performed by the local soundtrackers. It will be possible to appeal to the general court from a forward-looking decision. The compensations will be paid from the funds of the tenants or managers of the hunting circuit. There is a liquidation of the Compensation Fund, and the damage will be reported to the governor, not to the voivode. In addition, during the work of the subcommittee on the advice of the Legislative Office of the Sejm, the amendments contained in the article. The two projects which referred to the Law of 22 June 2016 amending the Law on Hunting Law were incorporated into the article. 1 and referred directly to the Law on Hunting Law, and the Act of 22 June 2016 will expire. This legislative procedure will certainly facilitate orientation in the amended legislation. High Izbo! At the stage of work, the joint committees introduced a package of amendments resulting from discussions and arrangements with the social side carried out by Minister Kowalczyk. These amendments provide for the real supervision of the Minister of the Environment of the Polish Łowiecki Union. In particular, these are these changes. Firstly, the Chief Executive Board shall report annually to the Minister for the year preceding 31 July. The Minister may request additional explanations and additions, he may order control of the Polish Łowiecki Union on the basis of the legality criterion, he may issue audit recommendations. The Polish Association of Hunters is required to send resolutions of the National Congress of Delegates and the Supreme Council of Hunters, which the Minister may repeal or which he may annul in whole or in part. The authorities of the Polish Łowiecki Union have a 5-year term, and two terms can be performed in the bodies of the Polish Łowiecki Union. The Minister shall appoint a national hunter from among the three candidates submitted by the Chief Hunter Council, and may also revoke him. At the request of the hunter, the national minister shall appoint from two to four members of the Chief Executive. The following article contains transitional provisions governing the rules and deadlines for the appointment and selection of new bodies of the Polish Łowiecki Union. The changes are deep. However, it should be noted that the Polish Hunting Union has the characteristics of a forced organisation similar to the self-government referred to in Article. 17 Constitution of the Republic of Poland, as stated in judgment K 21/11 of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Polish Hunting Union runs the hunting economy, while wild game belongs to the State Treasury. The Polish Hunter Union therefore manages resources that are our common good and should therefore be subject to control. The new control and surveillance instruments described above will strengthen the generational change, which has been taking place in the Polish Łowice Union for several years. The second issue, which is another issue regulated by these new amendments, is dezubekowanie. The bodies of the Polish Łowicki Union in accordance with the regulations that we have in the project and the management board of the hunting circle will not be able to include the person who was an employee, officer or soldier of the state security authorities. Persons born before 1 August 1972 will be required to make a illustrative statement under the procedure of the Mirror Act and the amendment amends the article. 4 and 8 of the Illustration Act concerning the verification of the accuracy of the illusory statement and the consequences of the misrepresentation in it. High Izbo! An important role in the functioning of the Polish Hunting Union and the proper hunting economy, in preparing young hunters to respect the principles of ethics, customs and hunting traditions, is played by who and on what principles sets the goals and directions of the Polish Hunting Union. This change of rules will allow the work of the Polish Łowiecki Union to be managed by people who, with their previous conduct, gave guarantee of integrity and decency. High Izbo! Another change is the possibility provided for the owner - it relates to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 10 July 2014, and I am talking about the state of the joint committee's report - making a written statement with a notarized signature on the prohibition of hunting on his property. It shall take effect from the day following the date of its service to the lessor of the hunting area - it shall be in accordance with the provisions of the Act of the Starost - or the Minister for the circuit excluded from the lease. Let me remind you that the OHZ have a slightly different regulation. Therefore, damage to property subject to prohibition of hunting will not be compensated. The establishment of the ban significantly limits the scope of the hunting economy, makes it difficult to reduce the number of game populations that cause hunting damage, so it obviously involves the intensity of payments, the scale of the hunting damage and the compensation associated with them. The withdrawal of the declaration of prohibition of real estate hunting may take place after the end of the hunting marketing year. I repeat: this is the situation recorded in the report of the Joint Committees, which does not yet exclude the possibility of modifying it. The fourth issue, which is included in these new amendments, is the clarification of the rules for reducing fire in national parks and nature reserves. I would like to remind you that the regulations on reducing fire in national parks and reserves have been in the Hunting Law for 22 years. This was usually done in such a way that the gunshots were recorded annually in the park's protective tasks and were most often carried out by a park worker. However, taking into account the expectations of the social side, they have been introduced in paragraph 1. 4, 5 and 6 in articles. 8 The hunting rights supplement, which provide for individual authorisations issued by the manager of the park or the regional environmental director, the supervision of the park worker or the DGOS in the performance of the reduction shot and the execution of such a shot in the first place by the national park staff. Such provisions shall be included in the report of the Joint Committees. The fifth issue included in these amendments is the introduction of rules that allow the Polish Hunting Union to take over the obligations of the hunting circle. In the event of the dissolution of the wheel, the Polish Association of Hunters is entitled to a refund claim concerning members of the Management Board of the circle, while in the event of non-payment within the deadline of compensation, the Polish Association of Hunters is entitled to a refund claim for the entire circle. Further rules to protect against possible insolvency, e.g. the compensation fund, may be established on the basis of the statute of the Polish Łowiecki Union. Here's another one. To date, the provision on obstruction of hunting contained in the report of the subcommittee in Article. 42aa, paragraph 13, concerning deliberate obstruction of hunting has yet to be mitigated. In the report of the Joint Committees, the entry concerns, I quote: deliberate obstruction of collective hunting, i.e. it is limited only to collective hunting, excluding forest, agricultural or fishing activities, the end of the quote. This is precisely the case at the moment in the report of the Joint Committees. The report of the joint committees shall specify a minimum distance for the shooting of the game. It has already been increased from 100 m to 150 m, but it does not rule out proceding on a subject that arouses great social emotions. The reason for adopting this compromise is the possibility of ensuring a sound hunting economy and responding to the progressive synanthropisation of the game, i.e. penetrating settlement areas in search of food. This record is also, so to speak, the subject of further discussion. Another change requires State Forests to organise training on the estimation of hunting damage, which was the responsibility of agricultural improvement centres in previous records. High Izbo! Parliamentary amendments were also introduced during the work of the joint committees. Amendment 8. Article 33a (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 1b: at least 50% local hunters in the hunting circle. I'd like to remind you that this was evoking a big social MRI. Farmers demanded, claimed that if there is a problem, the game does excessive damage and the population needs to be reduced, the most effective is the action of local hunters and hence this amendment, which meets the expectations of farmers. In turn, in amendment 14, Article 14. 46e in the paragraph. 4 was increased from 1 month to 3 months by the time limit for bringing an action before the General Court for damages. ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u264	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on amending the Law on Hunting Law and certain other laws. Primary printing is 1042 printing. The main objective of the proposed law is, in addition to the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, to introduce mechanisms to strengthen the supervision of the Minister responsible for environmental matters over the Polish Łowicki Union and to discipline the tenants and managers of hunting circuits in the implementation of annual hunting plans. The judgment of the Court states that the authorisation to take ownership of the property under the hunting circuit regime does not provide adequate legal means to protect the rights of the owner of the property. In the proposed law we take into account this and other doubts of the tribunal. Owners or users of perpetual property covered by the hunting circuit will be able to challenge the resolution of the voivodship sejm to the administrative court if it violates their legal interests. The proposed solutions adequately ensure the participation of owners and users of perpetual real estate in the process of dividing voivodship into hunting circuits. Owners or users of perpetual property covered by the hunting circuit will be able to make a written statement, with a notarially confirmed signature, of the prohibition of hunting on the designated property due to religious beliefs or moral principles. The project imposes information obligations on collective hunting. Owners and managers of hunting circuits will be required to inform in writing about planned collective hunting that is appropriate due to the place of hunting of mounds, mayors, city presidents and rivermen in good time. The draft provides that the owner, owner or landlord may object no later than 3 days before the planned date of commencement of the collective hunt. However, the objection will only be taken into account if hunting would endanger the safety or life of people. The project recognises a level playing field between tenants and managers of hunting circuits. The granting of the right of priority in the conclusion of the lease contract to the previous tenant for the following period shall be waived. The exercise of this right deprives the tenant of effective enforcement of the obligations incumbent on the tenant. It is proposed to require the Polish Hunting Union to provide environmental ministers with annual information from its activities and to extend the proposed supervisory regulations, including. the right to request resolutions and explanations as well as the right and possibility to impose a fine and to apply to the court for the repeal of the wrong or unlawful resolution of the Polish Łowicki Union. The project organizes mutual relations between the bodies of the Polish Łowiecki Union. The bill also provides for changes in the scope of judicial review in matters of acquisition and loss of membership of the Polish Łowiecki Union, the loss of membership of the circles and the related disciplinary penalties, which are foreseen for a member of the Polish Łowiecki Union. The changes are due to the divergence of case law that has arisen under the existing law. The project provides that all lease agreements in force on the date of entry into force will remain in force until the date of expiry of the division of voivodships into hunting circuits made before the date of entry into force of the Act. During the joint meeting of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, we introduced the principle that priority in the acceptance into the hunting circle, as Anna Paluch said earlier, has a member of the Polish Hunting Union residing in the area where the hunting region is leased, unless at least 50% members of a given circle shall be persons resident in that hunting area. Hunters living in the hunting area, including farmers above all, should have priority in the reception in the area where they have their land, where there are mainly hunting damage. Local hunters perform the tasks of the hunting economy more effectively than others. One of the main amendments introduced during the work of the subcommittee is the proposal for a completely new compensation mechanism. An assessment of the damage will be carried out by a team consisting of a representative of the executive body of the municipality's auxiliary unit, a representative of the tenant or a manager of the hunting circuit and a farmer. In the event of objections to the findings of the team made during the assessment of the injury, the member of the team will be able to raise these objections and to appeal to the relevant forward-looking party. The State Forestry Farm will conduct proceedings and determine the amount of compensation by means of a final actionable court. This solution satisfies everyone, gives additional administrative burden to the State Forest Forests. A particularly important change expected by the crowds of hunters, farmers and inhabitants of hunting grounds is that as of 1 April this year. from the authorities of the Polish Łowiecki Union, including hunting circles, officers of the security authorities of the Polish People's Republic will be eliminated. We've been waiting 27 years for this. Thanks to our determination - the present government - justice and security return. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development in print No 2234 on amending the Law on Hunting and certain other laws, the original draft was print No 1042. At the same time, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I make 11 amendments. The amendments now being tabled aim to take into account the demands of the social side, agricultural chambers and farmers, and to eliminate the doubts raised during the joint work of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry. I'm handing these amendments over to the marshal.

According to this thesis, the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development then proclaimed: hunting has never significantly reduced the population of wild boars in Europe. Action to significantly reduce the wild boar population may lead to the adaptation of wild boars and the increase in compensation of their population through influx from adjacent areas, e.g. from Belarus, increase their fertility and generally increase the risk of ASF spreading. This position was based, among others. in the 2014 EFSA report, Time and experience have shown how much it was a mistake, but some are still missing. In Germany and the Czech Republic, the countries at risk of spreading ASF have already decided to conduct collective hunting and depopulation of wild boars. ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u306	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill amending the Civil Code Act and some other laws. This proposal provides for amendments to the Civil Code Act, the Act of 9 April 2010 on the provision of economic information and the exchange of economic data, as regards limitation institutions, and the Law of 17 June 1966 on enforcement proceedings in the administration and the Act of 17 November 1964 on Civil Procedure. Amendments to the Civil Code Act. The first group concerns the principle of limitation of claims against consumers. The proposal provides that the statute of limitations on businesses' claims to consumers will result, by law, and not as a result of the consumer's complaint, in the form of the impossibility of effective redress. According to Art. 117 The Civil Code may, after considering the interests of both parties, fail to take into account the expiry of the limitation period for a claim against the consumer if the grounds of fairness so require. In exercising that right, the court should consider, in particular, the length of the limitation period, the length of the period from the expiry of the limitation period to the time of the claim, the nature of the circumstances which led to the failure of the entitled party to claim, including the impact of the behaviour of the obliged party on the delay in the recovery of the claim. The second group is to shorten the basic limitation periods. The proposal proposes a shorter general limitation period for claims - i.e. 6 years instead of the previous 10 years - while leaving the regulation that for interim and business claims the limitation period is 3 years. Thirdly, the method of calculating the expiry of the limitation period is changed, because in the proposed article. 118 The Civil Code provides that the limitation period shall end on the last day of the calendar year. Fourthly and lastly, as regards amendments to the Civil Code, we have a proposal to amend the provision of the Article. 568 § 2 The Civil Code, which will lead to the fact that in the case of the sale of used movable goods and the shortening by the parties of the warranty period the claims with the warranty will not be valid before the expiry of 2 years after the issue of the goods. The amendment to the Act on the provision of economic information and the exchange of economic data is a resultant one, with a reduction in the limitation period - from 10 years to 6 years. The amendments to the Enforcement Act in the Administration and the Civil Procedure Code are designed to ensure that the bank transfers the funds from the seized account to the bank account of the enforcement authority immediately after 7 days from the date of notification of the occupation. However, in the event of judicial execution of current maintenance or rent, the bank would transfer funds from the account seized to the bank account of the bailiff without delay. The last group of provisions are inter-temporal and introductory provisions. It is proposed to introduce a rule on the application of a new right to limitation of claims arising before the entry into force of the Act. Exceptions are provided for from this principle. The amended provisions are also intended to apply to enforcement proceedings pending on the date of entry into force of the Law. It is also proposed that the law should enter into force 30 days after the date of publication. This is in the most general outline of the content of the presented project. Thank you very much.

The unfairness of such a construction also involves making the limitation period conditional on the activities which the parties concerned may shape the legal situation according to their own will. For example, the creditor may request payment of the claim on purpose at the beginning of the year rather than at the end of the previous year and create at that time a liability from which the limitation period runs to extend the limitation period by one year. It is certainly not a sufficient reason for such an assumption that the time factor allegedly does not make it difficult for the debtor or the creditor to assess whether and when a pre-emptive claim is being made, because the finding of a legal event that is the beginning of the period is the source of doubt, rather than the calculation of the end of the period. Doubts, caused by legal qualifications rather than difficulties in evidence, can be exactly the same both when the deadline is one year and when it is longer. The claim that a creditor's faster recovery of claims will significantly increase the chance to recover money is not entirely true, as recovery depends first on the financial condition of the debtor and this can improve in the longer term. With regard to the amendment of Article. 125 § 1 c.c. with regard to shortening the deadline, it should be pointed out that the argument that the parties are being disciplined is not justified. After all, it is a question of claims, the condition of which has been established in appropriate proceedings. In such circumstances, any valuer rations are more likely to speak against a debtor who does not comply with the obligation arising from a judgment or settlement. The shortening of the limitation period, particularly severe in connection with the inter-temporal rules of the project, therefore constitutes a kind of expropriation of the creditor in the constitutional legal sense of the word and does not appear to be compatible with the interests of the security of legal trading. There may be different reasons why the creditor did not demand that the benefit be fulfilled immediately after it had become due. Very often there are cases where a debtor does not regulate liabilities despite the existence of an implementing title, and the assets hide it, for example by transferring it. to another person. It shows that its income is so low that the creditor is not able to satisfy it. On several occasions, a period of 10 years to enforce a claim allows creditors to seek the debtor's assets and to execute the debtor at the time when he collects the assets. It is difficult to guess what the legislator's objectives were in the absence of justification. Since, at first glance, the amendment proposed in the amendment raises such fundamental doubts, it can be imagined how many contradictory interpretations would lead to it being brought into legal order. The new rules contained in the project favour consumers to a large extent. In the government draft law, the proposed solutions are justified by the weaker position of the consumer and the need to provide him with the necessary protection. While ensuring consumer protection is a prerequisite that is always worth considering, this kind of distinction between business and consumer positions is debatable and contrary to the civilist principle of equality between entities and the law. This change is a genuine interference with civil law proceedings and unnecessarily violates the well-established principles of substantive law, since it implies a privileged position in the civil proceedings of a consumer who does not have to file a limitation charge, even though other entities are obliged to do so in order to benefit from this limitation. This change will also cause systemic reservations. The concept presented in the project refers to the pattern of codification from the Polish People's Republic, which was based on certain ideological goals. It may be argued that taking into account the expiry of the period of limitation from office will safeguard the interests of inefficient and ineffective debtors, it can be argued that the applicable civil procedure rules already prevent such persons from being protected today - e.g. Article 2 212 c.p.c. or the Consumer Credit Act. In addition, the above-mentioned justification for the project shows that the designer justifies in advance that the exercise of the debtor's right of entity should not be subject to his will. This conviction appears to be based on the assumption that, from the interests of the debtor, it will always be beneficial to statutoryly deprive the possibility of legal redress of pre-emptive claims without the need to make use of the statute of limitations. However, this assumption is also incorrect and results from a superficial analysis of the rule. The defendant may have a legitimate legal interest in ensuring that the period of validity is not taken into account in a situation where, in his view, the undertaking was not created or implemented and expired, while the obligation is extended by an existing obligation. Project solutions violate the principle of general retreat ban - lex retro non agit - which is a fundamental principle of a democratic rule of law. The provisions of the project significantly shorten the limitation period for claims that are already running. Retroactivity bans should be particularly respected when the proposed regulation worsens the situation of the individual, which is the case in the present case. The proposed rules are vague and can cause numerous interpretation doubts. Time for speaking does not allow to present a record after recording how these rules are inconsistent with each other. This not only violates the constitutional rules and the systemic wording of the Civil Code or other consumer laws, but also runs counter to the principles of legislative technique. And the Directive and the Regulation together provide a legal framework that regulates requirements for investment firms, regulated markets, service providers, information sharing and so-called companies of countries. third parties providing investment or investment services in the European Union. Both the Directive, the Regulation and the proposed draft law fall within the uniform right to apply in all the countries of the European Union. In the course of the work on this comprehensive document, the provisions of the Article were supplemented. 1 on numerous Commission Delegated Regulations, adopted in 2016 and 2017 as a complement to the basic Directive on technical standards for investment firms, market operators and credit institutions, trading venues or algorithmic trading investment firms. Substantive amendments have been introduced, inter alia. regulations enabling higher fees for speculators to be applied, the government debt management operations carried out also by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, i.e. not only by the Ministry, the deadlines for storage and archiving have been clarified, and banks have also been introduced in the same way as in brokerage houses, nomination committees or companies have been able to set flexible procedures and deadlines with distributors of information. Many controversy and discussions during the committee's work have raised the issue of functioning, among others. the nomination committee, whose tasks have not been detailed, and we say that the document is very extensive anyway. They have not been further defined in this draft law, so it is somewhat unfounded. On the other hand, we welcome this as a club, and so did the Committee on Public Finance, the proposal to reduce and set maximum fees for the management of investment funds by regulation, and I would like to draw attention to the very thorough analysis prepared by the Committee on Financial Supervision on this subject. The draft law sets out in very detail the scope of the powers and responsibilities of the KNF, these are obligations of a very extensive, administrative, supervisory, control, as well as penalties and sanctions for those who do not adapt. In this case, I must stress that the KNF has stood at the level of the task. In the assessment prepared, the committee draws attention to the need to interfere with the legislator and reduce the costs charged to investors of Polish investment funds. KNF considers that the Polish fund market requires legislative intervention as market regulation alone is insufficient and does not lead to lower costs for investors. Therefore, the fixing of maximum charges by the Minister of Finance is fully justified. Reducing the cost of investment funds will increase the level of protection of investors' interests, and in the longer term will result in improved performance and competitiveness compared to much cheaper foreign funds today. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In conclusion, the Polish People's Party Club - the European Union of Democrats welcomes the submitted project. Thank you very much.

On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a statement on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, prints No 2191 and 2277. The project concerns the necessary changes to the national legal order following the entry into force of European capital market regulations and will allow the application of EU rules on markets in financial instruments - MiFID II and MiFIR. The new solutions are designed to increase the competitiveness and efficiency of financial markets in Poland and the European Union and to increase the safety of participants. The Commission of Public Finances on 26 February 2018 introduced numerous amendments to the draft law consisting, inter alia, of the clarification referred to in the text of the act. As the projectors point out in the explanatory memorandum, these provisions will improve transparency of trading for participants in capital instruments markets and increase transparency of turnover for non-equity markets. Investment firms providing investment consulting services will be required, among others. to provide information on whether they provide services in an independent manner and to disclose the costs of their advice and explain the basis for the advice provided. In addition, there will be a limitation on the possibility of receiving or collecting remuneration, commissions or any monetary or non-monetary advantage from third parties, in particular from issuers or suppliers of products by companies providing independent investment advice and portfolio management services. However, the bill raises doubts in several points. Firstly, as noted by the Confederation of Leviatan, the provisions in question are very vague and introduce solutions according to which portfolio management companies are fully aligned in terms of such activities with investment firms. This is not the result of the MiFID II Directive or Regulation 2017/565. Secondly, as many stakeholders have pointed out in the consultation on this project, some provisions have not been agreed and, more importantly, they also do not constitute the implementation of any provisions of Community law. In view of the above, the Modern is in favour of introducing an amendment to remove the article. 32b. Four fund laws. That's the amendment, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u384	Today, the Committee on Culture and Media discussed this draft resolution commemorating the century of the death of the president of the city of Cracow Juliusz Lea. Juliusz Leo was a lawyer, economist, professor of the Jagiellonian University, politician, self-government officer, Member of the National Sejm of Galicia, Member of the Austrian Council of State. For 16 years he served as the president of the city of Krakow, he was also the first president of the National Committee and the initiator of the creation of Polish Legions. His presidency is a period of development of Krakow, not just a geographical one. The city under his rule grew to 47 km2 from approx. 6 km2. In this respect, Zwierzyniec, Krowodrze, Prądnik Czerwony, Prądnik Biały, Grzegórzki, Dąbie, Płaszów and Podgórze joined the agreement with representatives of 13 villages and suburban communes. For comparison, the area of Warsaw was 33 km2. The Great Kraków Juliusz Lea is also a quality development of the city, thanks to which Kraków became a modern European metropolis - regulation of the banks of the Vistula River, the shift of the Rudava trough, the extension of the sewage system, the water supply network and the extension of the communication network with new tram lines, the orderly financial management of the city and the increase of its revenue, the construction of the third bridge on the Vistula River, as well as the construction of the city power station and the creation of a new freight station. He served as President of Krakow until his death. He died on 21 February 1918. [[Applause]] [[Władysław Kosiniak-Kamish: Bravo! We're in favour.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u397	Thank you very much. Mr Wojciech Buczak, Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u398	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This year we are celebrating the 37th anniversary of the signing of the crowd-stranger agreements. On the night of 18 to 19 February 1981 in Rzeszów agreements were signed between representatives of the communist government of the Polish People's Republic and the striking farmers and supporting their representatives of the NSZZ A day later similar agreements were concluded in the Lower mouths. These agreements, known under the common name of the Rzeszow-Sztrzych agreements, were the fourth nationwide agreements - after Szczecin, Gdańsk and Jastrzębski - which the communist authorities signed with independent representatives of Polish society, and the first ones in which the demands of villagers were taken into account. The agricultural strike in Podkarpacie began on 29 December 1980 the occupation of the building of the City and Municipal Office in Ustrzyki Dolne. Bieszczadzki farmers provoked protest, among others. announcement of forced displacement in connection with the construction of a government center in Arlamów. On January 12, 1981, protesters in the Lower mouths removed the militia by force. At that time, the strikers moved to Rzeszow and joined the protest of representatives of the workers occupying the building of the Kolejarz House - the seat of the disbanded regional council of trade unions. The strikers were supported by the National Commission of NSZZ and the Catholic Church led by Fr. Archbishop Ignatius Tokarczuk. Just recently we celebrated the 100th anniversary of the birth of this great priest, the unbreakable bishop. Several thousand people took part in the strike that lasted over a month and a half. Local activists were joined by representatives of peasant organizations from other regions of Poland. On 18 February, the agreement was finally negotiated in Rzeszów. The protocol containing the determination was signed shortly after midnight on 19 February 1981. In the agreements farmers obtained guarantees of non-infringement of farm ownership and inheritance rights, equal treatment of individual farmers and cooperative agriculture, ensuring the abolition of restrictions on the marketing of agricultural land. A number of worldview provisions were also included, among others. concerning freedom in religious construction and access to religious practices, the extension of schools and kindergartens in rural areas has also been declared. The peasant strike ended with the signing of the Rzeszow-Sztrzych agreements contributed to the establishment and registration of NSZZ (Solidarity of Individual Farmers). The original copy of the Rzeszow-Ostrzyckie agreements was transferred personally in 2006 to the Jasna Góra Pro Patria Foundation during the National Pilgrimage of Labour People to Jasna Góra. High Izbo! Today, in view of the historical importance of the throng-string agreements, already under new systemic conditions, let us ensure the sustainable development of the whole country, including in rural areas. The fight against striking farmers in 1980 and 1981 is an obligation for us to protect the wealth of the Polish village and defend the Polish land. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u399	Thank you very much. Mrs Barbara Dziuk, Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u400	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In a moment, the law on public disclosure has entered into force, leading to opposition from certain backgrounds. The letters of the local government calling for the help of the Members on this bill were sent to my parliamentary office. The bill raises understandable concerns. It provides for the creation of an open register of civil law contracts in the public sector, proposes a new model of open declarations of property, imposes an obligation to keep a register of civil law contracts, including units of the public finance sector. It assumes transparency and responsibility for decisions made, giving published property statements a mass character. It also includes data on spouses of persons in management positions performing public functions. Based on the experience of other European countries, the Act establishes and regulates the status of a whistleblower, a man who will obtain professional and legal protection for information on corruption. The Act also sets out a list of persons whose statements will be made public. These will be statements of property of persons holding managerial positions in various state institutions. All of these procedures are designed to reduce practices and irregularities, among others. financial, treatment and benefits obtained by means of positions occupied. The Act will very quickly introduce legal order where it has not yet been achieved. In our country, everyone has eyes and sees a lot, and even a lot, but so far there has been no legal mechanism that would clear and fairly eliminate public corruption and point to irregularities in the management of state assets, that is, our common. I thank you on behalf of all Poles and Poles for this bill, because we are all equal to the law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

A single parent with one child earning 1601 PLN does not get paid for support from a maintenance fund or 500+. It cannot be that the minimum wage will result in a lack of child support from the maintenance fund. Under the current conditions, the minimum wage is growing slower than many of the first-needed products. We cannot look at inflation, i.e. rising prices throughout the economy. Let's take a look at the rise in the prices of first-needed articles. Maintaining the threshold for the use of the maintenance fund for 10 years at the same level makes it possible for people who really need financial assistance to receive benefits. ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u405	Thank you. Mr Bogusław Sonik, Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-02-27-sejm-59-1.u406	On 2 May 2017, the 120th anniversary of the birth of Adolf Hyła, a Polish painter who was the author of one of the most famous images of Jesus the Merciful Jesus, I trust you. Adolf Hyła was born on 2 May 1897 in Biała near Bielska. At the age of 15, he joined the Jesuits College in the Old Village, where he received lower religious ordinations. After his graduation, he studied philosophy at the Jagiellonian University. Drawing and painting he studied with Jack Malczewski. In his painting he focused mainly on religious subjects. He is also the author of several dozen portraits, including family portraits, self-portraits and the image of Brother Albert, as well as many Tatra landscapes. However, the most famous work of Adolf Hyła is the painting depicting Jesus the Merciful Jesus, I trust you, which is located in the religious chapel at the Sanctuary of Divine Mercy in Krakow-Łagiewniki. Based on the description provided by St. Faustina in the Diary and talks with Faustina Kowalska's Confessor Adolf Hyła painted a painting of the Merciful Jesus. This image, along with the worship of God's mercy, spread throughout the world. It is one of the most famous paintings of Jesus the Resurrection. About the painting of Adolf Hyła in 1997 in Łagiewniki, John Paul II said: "Everyone can come here, look at this image of the merciful Christ, at his heart radiating with graces and deep down to hear what he has heard - do not be afraid of anything, I am always with you. And if he answers Jesus with a sincere heart, I trust in you, he will find comfort in all anxiety and fear. The images of Jesus the Merciful by Adolf Hyla are all over the world and are an important part of the worship of God's mercy. So let us pay tribute to the Polish painter, whose painting work is the most recognizable and widely distributed picture of Jesus the Merciful. [[Applause]]
